an open-source JavaScript library
for building powerful interactive maps

  • Overview
  • Tutorials
  • Docs
  • Download
  • Plugins
  • Blog
  • GitHub

Leaflet Plugins database

These are plugins for Leaflet, which Maplair is a fork of. None of them is compatible with Maplair: they import from leaflet and expect leaflet-* CSS classes and the L global. Many of their features are part of Maplair, and the list may help to port a plugin.

Tile & image layers

  • Basemap providers
  • Basemap formats
  • Non-map base layers
  • Tile/image display
  • Tile load
  • Vector tiles

Overlay data

  • Overlay data formats
  • Dynamic data loading
  • Synthetic overlays
  • Data providers

Overlay Display

  • Markers & renderers
  • Overlay animations
  • Clustering/decluttering
  • Heatmaps
  • DataViz

Overlay interaction

  • Edit geometries
  • Time & elevation
  • Search & popups
  • Area/overlay selection

Map interaction

  • Layer switching controls
  • Interactive pan/zoom
  • Bookmarked pan/zoom
  • Fullscreen
  • Minimaps & synced maps
  • Measurement
  • Mouse coordinates
  • Events
  • User interface
  • Print/export
  • Geolocation

Miscellaneous

  • Geoprocessing
  • Routing
  • Geocoding
  • Plugin collections

Integration

  • Frameworks & build systems
  • 3rd party
Develop your own

Tile & image layers

The following plugins allow loading different maps and provide functionality to tile and image layers.

  • Basemap providers
  • Basemap formats
  • Non-map base layers
  • Tile/image display
  • Tile load
  • Vector tiles

Basemap providers

Ready-to-go basemaps, with little or no configuration at all.

<tr>
	<td><a class="plugin-repo-url" href="https://github.com/Azure-Samples/azure-maps-leaflet">Azure Maps Leaflet plugin</a></td>
	<td>
		<p>A leafletjs plugin that makes it easy to overlay all the different tile layers available from the <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/services/azure-maps/">Azure Maps</a>. Supports using an Azure Maps subscription key or Azure Active Directory for authentication.</p>

Plugin Description V1 V2 Demo Maintainer

Basemap formats

Plugins for loading basemaps or GIS raster layers in common (albeit non-default) formats.

<tr>
	<td><a class="plugin-repo-url" href="https://github.com/allmaps/allmaps/tree/main/packages/leaflet">allmaps-leaflet</a></td>
	<td>
		<p>Allmaps plugin for Leaflet. This plugin allows displaying georeferenced <a href="https://iiif.io/">IIIF</a> images on a Leaflet map. 100,000s of (historical) maps are available through IIIF, across libraries, archives and museums worldwide. The plugin works by loading <a href="https://preview.iiif.io/api/georef/extension/georef/">Georeference Annotations</a> and uses WebGL to transform images from a IIIF image server to overlay them on their correct geographical position. See <a href="https://www.allmaps.org">allmaps.org</a> for more information.</p>

		<div class="repo-data"></div>
	</td>
	<td class="compatible">
	
		<span title="Compatible with V1">✔️</span>
	
	</td>
	<td class="compatible">
	
	</td>
	<td>
	
		<a href="https://observablehq.com/@allmaps/leaflet-plugin">Demo</a>
	
	</td>
	<td>
	
		<a href="https://www.allmaps.org">Allmaps</a>
	
	</td>
</tr>

Plugin Description V1 V2 Demo Maintainer

Tile/image display

The following plugins change the way that tile or image layers are displayed in the map.

<tr>
	<td><a class="plugin-repo-url" href="https://github.com/valkenburg/Leaflet.Control.DetailLevel">Leaflet.Control.DetailLevel</a></td>
	<td>
		<p>Display tiles at higher-than-retina (hdpi) resolutions, by real-time modification of the zoomOffset. Useful for mapping sources which drastically change map style between different zoom levels. Increasing the zoomOffset by too much does slow down the browser, as the number of displayed tiles grows exponentially with the zoomOffset.</p>

		<div class="repo-data"></div>
	</td>
	<td class="compatible">
	
		<span title="Compatible with V1">✔️</span>
	
	</td>

Overlay data

The following plugins provide new ways of loading overlay data (GIS vector data): points, lines and polygons.

  • Overlay data formats
  • Dynamic data loading
  • Synthetic overlays
  • Data providers

Plugin Description V1 V2 Demo Maintainer

Overlay display

The following plugins provide new ways of displaying overlay data information.

  • Markers & renderers
  • Overlay animations
  • Clustering/decluttering
  • Heatmaps
  • DataViz

Markers & renderers

These plugins provide new markers or news ways of converting abstract data into images in your screen. Maplair users versed in GIS also know these as symbolizers.

	<td>
		<p>Extends <a href="https://github.com/EricDalnas/leaflet.regions">leaflet.regions</a> to render world country boundaries using ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 keys. Supports per-country color, permanent labels, click/hover popups, and custom label positions for countries with non-centered centroids. Handles Natural Earth’s ISO_A2 <code>-99</code> fallback automatically. Use <code>defaultVisible: false</code> to highlight only specific countries.</p>

	<td><a class="plugin-repo-url" href="https://github.com/EricDalnas/leaflet.regions">leaflet.regions</a></td>
	<td>
		<p>Base plugin for rendering GeoJSON regions (polygons) with per-feature color, permanent labels, and click/hover popups. Supports data binding via a simple key→object map, <code>defaultVisible: false</code> for focused single-region displays, and live re-render via <code>refresh()</code>. Extended by <a href="https://github.com/EricDalnas/leaflet.us-states">leaflet.us-states</a> and <a href="https://github.com/EricDalnas/leaflet.countries">leaflet.countries</a>.</p>

Plugin Description V1 V2 Demo Maintainer

Interaction with geometries/features

The following plugins enable users to interact with overlay data: edit geometries, select areas or features, interact with the time dimension, search features and display information about them.

  • Edit geometries
  • Time & elevation
  • Search & popups
  • Area/overlay selection

Edit geometries

Allows users to create, draw, edit and/or delete points, lines and polygons.

<tr>

Draw, Edit, Drag, Cut, Rotate, Split, Scale, Measure, Snap and Pin Layers like Markers, CircleMarkers, Polylines, Polygons, Circles, Rectangles, ImageOverlays, Text, LayerGroups, GeoJSON, MultiLineStrings and MultiPolygons. Supports holes in polygons, canvas mode and more.</p>

Plugin Description V1 V2 Demo Maintainer

Time & elevation

Most data is two-dimensional (latitude and longitude), but some data has more dimensions (altitude and/or time). The following plugins help users navigate these extra dimensions.

<tr>
	<td><a class="plugin-repo-url" href="https://github.com/carlosign/LeafletSlider-LocalTimeV2">LeafletSlider-LocalTimeV2</a></td>
	<td>
		<p>LeafletSlider-LocalTimeV2  enables you to dynamically add and remove Markers (without lag) on a map, and show the daterange in localtime by using a JQuery UI slider</p>

		<div class="repo-data"></div>
	</td>
	<td class="compatible">
	
		<span title="Compatible with V1">✔️</span>
	
	</td>

Plugin Description V1 V2 Demo Maintainer

Map interaction

New ways to interact with the map itself.

  • Layer switching controls
  • Interactive pan/zoom
  • Bookmarked pan/zoom
  • Fullscreen
  • Minimaps & synced maps
  • Measurement
  • Mouse coordinates
  • Events
  • User interface
  • Print/export
  • Geolocation

Routing

The following plugins use external services to calculate driving or walking routes.

<tr>
	<td><a class="plugin-repo-url" href="https://github.com/traffordDataLab/leaflet.reachability">Leaflet.Reachability</a></td>
	<td>
		<p>Show areas of reachability based on time or distance for different modes of travel using the <a href="https://openrouteservice.org/dev/#/api-docs">openrouteservice isochrones API</a>.</p>

		<div class="repo-data"></div>
	</td>
	<td class="compatible">
	
		<span title="Compatible with V1">✔️</span>

	</td>
</tr>

<tr>
	<td><a class="plugin-repo-url" href="https://github.com/StephanGeorg/leaflet-routeboxer">Leaflet RouteBoxer</a></td>
	<td>
		<p>This is a Leaflet implementation of the RouteBoxer Class from Google. The Leaflet RouteBoxer class generates a set of L.LatLngBounds objects that are guaranteed to cover every point within a specified distance of a path.</p>

		<div class="repo-data"></div>

Uses <a href="http://project-osrm.org/">OSRM</a> by default, but also supports<a href="https://docs.mapbox.com/api/">Mapbox Directions API</a>.</p>

		<p>Retrieve all Leaflet Default Icon options from CSS, in particular all icon images URL’s, to improve compatibility with bundlers and frameworks that modify URL’s in CSS. In particular for webpack (with style-, css-, file- and url-loader’s), Rails Asset Pipeline and Django pipeline. Should solve all use cases linked to <a href="https://github.com/Leaflet/Leaflet/issues/4968">issue Leaflet/Leaflet #4968</a>. <a href="https://ghybs.github.io/leaflet-defaulticon-compatibility/webpack-demo.html">Demo with webpack</a> (and <a href="https://ghybs.github.io/leaflet-defaulticon-compatibility/webpack-demo.html?demo=no-plugin">without this plugin</a>).</p>

		<div class="repo-data"></div>
	</td>
	<td class="compatible">
	
		<span title="Compatible with V1">✔️</span>
	
	</td>
	<td class="compatible">
	
	</td>
	<td>
	
		<a href="https://ghybs.github.io/leaflet-defaulticon-compatibility/webpack-demo.html">Demo</a>

Plugin Description V1 V2 Demo Maintainer

Develop your own

Maplair keeps it simple. If you can think of a feature that is not required by all Maplair users, and you can write the JavaScript code in a reusable way, you’ve got yourself a Maplair plugin already.

There are no hard requirements on how to create your own plugin, but all developers are encouraged to read the recommendations in the plugin guide.

Once your plugin is ready, you can submit it: just send a pull request with a new plugin file in /docs/_plugins/to our GitHub repository.
